Um, well... I guess it's been a while. I really hope I don't sound like I'm just whining, but... it's been about two years, maybe two years and a couple of months? 

I think it started right around the time the clinic changed how they do staffing, and my schedule just started rotating all over the place. I really try to be flexible and go wherever they need me, because I know they count on me, but... yeah, it's just been kind of hard to adjust since then. 

I'm sorry, I should probably be used to the schedule by now, right? I don't want to make it sound like it's their fault or anything. I just... that's when I noticed the heavy feeling really starting to stick around. Does that timeline make sense to you?

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... I guess I still try to do the things I usually do. Like, I still read a bit, and I try to keep up with my friends when I can. I don't want to be a burden or stop doing things just because I feel a little down, you know? 

But... if I'm being really honest... I guess it doesn't feel quite the same. It's like I'm just going through the motions so people don't worry about me. I mean, I don't want to sound ungrateful for the things I have, because I know I have a lot to be thankful for. It's just... the spark isn't really there right now. 

I'm sorry, I know I should probably be trying harder to enjoy things. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too negative.

Um... well, I really try my best to stay on top of everything, especially at work because it's so important. I don't want you to think I'm doing a bad job or putting anyone at risk, because I really do care about the patients.

Um... well, I guess that's actually kind of why I finally came in. I really hate to complain, especially because, like you said, nothing bad is really happening around me. I don't want you to think I'm just being ungrateful or that I don't appreciate my life.

I guess I came in because, well, for a really long time now, I just... I wake up already feeling kind of low. I don't want to sound like I'm just complaining or being negative, but it's like there’s this heavy, gray feeling on me before I even get to the clinic for work. And it pretty much just follows me through the whole day. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any loss of interest or pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, such as hobbies, work, or spending time with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really? I mean... I still try to keep up with everything. I still go to work, and I definitely still care about seeing my family and my friends. I don't want to let anybody down, you know? So I make sure I still do all the things we usually do. \n\nI guess I just... I do them while feeling kind of heavy. But I haven't stopped liking my hobbies or anything like that. I still want to do them. \n\nI hope that's a helpful answer? I'm sorry if it's not what you were looking for... is it okay that I still enjoy things?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you feel fatigued or lacking in energy most days, even after a full night's sleep?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess I do struggle with that a little bit. I really don't want to sound lazy or anything, because I promise I always try my best at work... but even after a day off, or if I sleep in late, my body just still feels kind of worn out. \n\nBy the time I finish rooming all the patients at the clinic and catching up on my charts, it just feels like I have absolutely nothing left in the tank. I'll get home and I can't even get myself to do my laundry, or go shoot hoops, or even mess around with my electronics stuff like I usually would. \n\nI just feel bad because I'm only twenty, so I feel like I should have a lot more energy, you know? I hope it's okay that I'm admitting that. Does that answer your question all right?\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble falling asleep, staying asleep, or have you been sleeping much more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, my sleep is actually pretty okay, I think. I mean, I don't want to make a big deal out of it or anything. I usually fall asleep all right, and I don't think I'm sleeping too much... I definitely still make sure I'm up in time for my shifts at the clinic. \n\nI guess I just sleep a normal amount? I hope that's a good thing. I try really hard to keep a good schedule so I'm not dragging too much at work. Is that the kind of answer you needed?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling worthless or guilty, maybe blaming yourself for things more than usual, even when others wouldn't expect you to?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I try really hard to do a good job at work and be a good friend, so I don't think I have a reason to feel worthless. I know I'm not perfect or anything, but I always try to pull my weight. \n\nI guess sometimes if I mess up at work, like if I forget to stock a room or something, I feel bad about it, but... I think that's normal, right? I just want to make sure I'm doing things right and not making things harder for the nurses or the doctors. \n\nI don't really sit around feeling guilty for no reason, though. I hope that makes sense? I'm sorry if I'm not explaining it very well. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety, without being able to settle down?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I've noticed anything like that. I mean, I try to stay pretty steady, especially at the clinic. You kind of have to be, right? \n\nI definitely don't think I'm moving super slow or anything... I always try to hustle and keep up with the pace so I don't slow anyone else down. And I'm not really the fidgety type either. I try to stay focused on what I'm doing. \n\nI guess I just feel pretty normal when it comes to how I move and talk. I hope that's what you were asking? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. Is that a helpful answer?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you feel down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, even when nothing particularly bad is happening?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe that's actually kind of why I came in. I don't want to sound like I'm complaining, because I know I have a good job and everything, but... yeah. \n\nFor a really long time now, I just kind of wake up already feeling... low? Like, before I even leave for the clinic, there's just this heavy, gray feeling on me. And it pretty much follows me through the whole day. I try really hard to push through it and put on a good face for the patients and the staff, but it's just always kind of there in the background. \n\nI hope it's okay that I'm admitting that. I don't want you to think I'm not trying my best or that I'm ungrateful for my life. It's just... hard sometimes. Is that the kind of thing you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way most days — this low or heavy feeling that you described?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it's been a while. I really hope I don't sound like I'm just whining, but... it's been about two years, maybe two years and a couple of months? \n\nI think it started right around the time the clinic changed how they do staffing, and my schedule just started rotating all over the place. I really try to be flexible and go wherever they need me, because I know they count on me, but... yeah, it's just been kind of hard to adjust since then. \n\nI'm sorry, I should probably be used to the schedule by now, right? I don't want to make it sound like it's their fault or anything. I just... that's when I noticed the heavy feeling really starting to stick around. Does that timeline make sense to you?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this way before — like there’s a heavy, low feeling that sticks around — or is this the first time you’ve experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, growing up and everything, I was usually pretty okay. I'd have bad days, like anyone else, of course—I don't want to sound like I was perfect or anything. But this kind of... constant, heavy feeling? No, I really don't think I've ever felt quite like this before. \n\nBefore all the schedule changes at the clinic, I felt like I had a pretty good handle on things. I was, you know, doing my job and feeling alright about it. So I'm pretty sure this is the first time it's been this bad. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right history so you can do your job. Am I making sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or extremely confident — maybe even so much that it interfered with your sleep or caused you to act impulsively?\n\nPatient: Um, no... no, definitely not. I really hope I don't give the impression that I'm, you know, out of control or anything like that. I've never felt unusually high or energetic like that. \n\nI mean, I try to be upbeat at work for the patients, but it's never been something where I couldn't sleep or acted impulsively. I'm actually a pretty cautious person, maybe even a little too boring sometimes? I just try to keep my head down and do what I'm supposed to do. \n\nI've never had anything like what you're describing. I hope that's okay? I just want to be sure I'm answering right.\n\nDoctor: Even if you still do the things you used to enjoy, do you find that you no longer get the same sense of pleasure or satisfaction out of them as you used to?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I still try to do the things I usually do. Like, I still read a bit, and I try to keep up with my friends when I can. I don't want to be a burden or stop doing things just because I feel a little down, you know? \n\nBut... if I'm being really honest... I guess it doesn't feel quite the same. It's like I'm just going through the motions so people don't worry about me. I mean, I don't want to sound ungrateful for the things I have, because I know I have a lot to be thankful for. It's just... the spark isn't really there right now. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I should probably be trying harder to enjoy things. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too negative.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble concentrating on things, like work tasks, reading, or following conversations, even though you're trying your best to focus?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try my best to stay on top of everything, especially at work because it's so important. I don't want you to think I'm doing a bad job or putting anyone at risk, because I really do care about the patients. \n\nBut... yeah, I guess I have been having a little trouble lately. Like, sometimes I'll be staring at a patient's intake screen, and I just... I have to reread the same line over and over before it actually registers. And, um, I've caught myself missing really little details, like a medication dose, or I'll just completely mix up what I was about to ask the patient next. \n\nI always catch it, though! I promise I'm being really careful. It's just taking me a lot more effort to stay focused than it used to. I feel really bad about it... I hope that doesn't make me sound incompetent? I'm trying really hard to push through it. Is that normal for what we're talking about?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood feels depressed most of the day, nearly every day, even when nothing particularly bad is happening around you?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess that's actually kind of why I finally came in. I know you asked something similar just a little bit ago, and I don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm not listening.
